QUESTION PRESENTED 


Whether this Court should decline the invitation of 
the New Jersey Supreme Court to decide whether New 
Jersey’s hate crime law, N.J.S.A. 2C:44-3e., unconstitu- 
tionally provides for an extended term of imprisonment 
increasing the maximum possible penalty by ten years, 
based on proof by a preponderance of the evidence, 
rather than proof beyond a reasonable doubt, and denies 
the defendant rights to notice by indictment and trial by 


jury.

JURISDICTION 


The petitioner, Charles C. Apprendi, Jr., respectfully 
prays that a writ of certiorari be issued to review the June 
24, 1999 judgment and opinion of the Supreme Court of 
New Jersey affirming the decision of the Superior Court 
of New Jersey, Appellate Division, affirming his convic- 
tion and sentence under a hate crime law, N./J.S.A. 
2C:44-3e., originally entered by Superior Court of New 
Jersey, Law Division, Judge Rushton Ridgway. 


The jurisdiction of this court is invoked under 28 
U.S.C. § 1257(a).
